THIS WRIT PETITION (CIVIL) HAVING COME UP FOR ADMISSION ON 08.03.2023,
ALONG WITH WP(C).14051/2022 AND CONNECTED CASES, THE COURT ON
21.03.2023 DELIVERED THE FOLLOWING:

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 115 :
 Dated this the 21st day of March, 2023.
 JUDGMENT
Batch I:
[W.P.(C) Nos. 14051, 14076, 14145, 15241, 15275, 15277, 15510, 15661,

 —----------------------------
The captioned writ petitions are in respect of tenders invited
by the Kerala Water Authority; the challenge therein is basically in
respect of the Additional Performance Guarantee required to be
furnished in the context of various State Government Orders, and
memoranda of Government of India issued during Covid 19

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 116 :
pandemic; and orders of the State Government granting relaxation
in the matter of furnishing performance guarantee under certain
circumstances. Therefore, it is for convenience that they are divided
into different batches; but at the same time, common contentions
are raised claiming benefits of the Government Orders/
memoranda.
2. I have heard learned counsel for the petitioners, Sri.
Santhosh Mathew, Sri. George Mathew, Sri. V. Premchand, Sri.
Shiraz Abdulla, Sri. Tessy Jose, Sri. Shanes Methar and the other
learned Advocates, the learned Standing Counsel for the Kerala
Water Authority, Sri. V.V. Joshy, Sri. P.M. Johny and Sri. Georgie
Johny, Central Government Counsel Smt. Mini Gopinath, and the
learned Special Government Pleader Sri K. V. Manoj Kumar, and
perused the pleadings and material on record. I also place on record
that since other writ petitions of the like nature are pending, other
learned counsel who offered to advance arguments, were also
heard.
Batch 1:
3. The captioned Batch 1 writ petitions are with respect to
the tenders invited by the Kerala Water Authority to carry out its
civil works; among other conditions, furnishing of Additional

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 117 :
Performance Guarantee is a term of the contract, out of which at
least 50% shall be collected in the form of treasury fixed deposit
and the rest in the form of bank guarantee or any other forms
prescribed in the revised PWD Manual. The question to be decided
is whether such a condition is valid in law, in view of certain orders
issued by the State Government, and Office Memoranda issued by
the Government of India in the context of Covid-19 Pandemic.
4. According to the petitioners, by virtue of the Order dated
07.01.2021 issued by the State Government, the requirements of
the Performance Security/Security Deposit, Bid Security/Earnest
Money Deposit and Additional Performance Guarantee for the
execution of the public works in the State were relaxed taking into
account Covid-19 pandemic situation, operative for a period of one
year. The period of relaxation for furnishing the Performance
Guarantee for the execution of the public works in the State alone
was extended as per Government Order, G.O.(P) No. 32/2022/Fin.
dated 15.03.2022 for a further period up to 31.03.2023.
5. On a perusal of the Government Order dated 07.01.2021,
it is clear that the Additional Performance Guarantee is the
additional amount to be deposited for unbalanced price i.e. for
works quoted below the estimate rate; and Additional Performance
Guarantee will be required, if the works quoted between 11% to

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 118 :
25% below the estimate rate. Government Order, G.O.(P) No.
01/2019/FIN. dated 10.01.2019 stipulates that if the quoted
amount of the lowest bidder (L1) is x% below the estimated PAC,
the Additional Performance Guarantee shall be (X-10%) of
estimated PAC. The said order was issued by the State Government
on account of slowdown in economy and acute financial crunch
among the contractors due to the pandemic Covid-19.
6. It is true, the Government of India issued guidelines as per
Office Memoranda bearing Nos. F18/4/2020-PPD and F9/4/2020-
PPD dated 13.05.2020 and 12.11.2020 respectively and the State
Governments were requested to consider issuing similar instructions
in respect of procurement by the State Government, State
Government Public Sector Undertakings, Local Bodies and all
agencies controlled by them. As per the aforesaid Office
Memoranda, the Government of India issued certain guidelines that
(i) reduction in Performance Security exceeding 5-10% to 3% of the
value of the contract; (ii) no provisions regarding Bid Security
should be kept in the Bid Documents in future and only provisions
for Bid Security Declaration should be kept in the Bid Documents;
and (iii) no provision should be kept in the Bid Documents regarding
the Additional Security Deposit/Bank Guarantee in case of
Abnormally Low Bids. Other guidelines were also issued by the

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 119 :
Central Government.
7. The State Government examined the matter taking into
account the request made by the Government of India and ordered
that (1) Performance Security/ Security Deposit to be submitted at
the time of executing the agreement is reduced from the existing
rate of 5% to 3% of the contract amount; (2) correspondingly Bid
Security/Earnest Money Deposit is reduced from 2.50% to 1.50% of
the estimated amount; (3) Additional Performance Guarantee is
waived for the low quoted items on the condition that the bidder
shall furnish an undertaking to execute all low quoted items in full
as per contract terms; (4) the above relaxations on account of
Covid-19 pandemic are made applicable for works initially for one
year and would be reviewed post that date; and (5) the
Government Order is made applicable to all new tenders as well as
works which have been tendered and awarded, but agreements
have not been signed by the winning bidder.
8. Later, the State Government, by order bearing No. G.O.(P)
No. 32/2022/Fin.Dated 15.03.2022, considered the subject matter,
taking into account the Office Memorandum No.F 9/4/2020-PPD
dated 30.12.2021 issued by the Government of India, and decided
to extend the benefits of reducing the rate of Performance
Guarantee to contracts executed under the State of Kerala.

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 120 :
Accordingly, the State Government extended the relaxations in
Performance Guarantee ordered as per the Government Order
dated 07.01.2021 alone for a further period upto 31.03.2023.
Therefore, it can be seen that even though various relaxations were
granted by the State Government as per Government Order dated
07.01.2021, the said relaxations were altered by the Government
Order dated 15.03.2022 by confining the relaxation in respect of the
Performance Guarantee alone as ordered in the Government Order
dated 07.01.2021.
9. The paramount contention advanced by the petitioners in
the writ petitions is that the Office Memorandum issued by the
Government of India relaxing the conditions during pandemic
period is binding on the State Government; and therefore, the State
Government was duty bound to exempt furnishing of Additional
Performance Guarantee, since the Government of India have not
provided any time limit for the relaxation ordered in its
memorandum.
10. On an analysis of the Office Memoranda of the
Government of India, I find that the relaxations due to pandemic
Covid-19 was granted by the Government of India consequent to
the representations received by the Government on account of
slowdown in economy due to pandemic and also due to acute

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 121 :
financial crunch among many commercial entities and contractors,
which, in turn, was affecting timely execution of the contract.
Therefore, in all contracts, the Performance Security was reduced
from the existing rate of 5-10% to 3% of the value of the contract
for all existing contracts. Further, all tenders/contracts issued or
collected till 31.12.2021 were given the benefit of the reduced
Performance Security. Various other benefits were also granted by
the Government of India.
11. It was made clear in those Office Memoranda that the
instructions will be applicable for all kinds of procurement namely
goods, Consultancy, works, non consulting services etc. and are
issued under Rule 6(1) of the General Financial Rules (GFRs), 2017.
The Office Memoranda further shows that it was issued to all the
Secretaries and Financial Advisers to the Government of India and
copy to the Secretary, Department of Public Enterprises with a
request to issue the same instructions to Central Public Sector
Undertakings. It is also clear that the Central Government, in its
Office Memorandum dated 12.11.2020, has relaxed the condition of
Additional Performance Guarantee in case of Abnormally Low Bids,
however, with a rider that wherever there are compelling
circumstances to ask for Additional Security Deposit/Bank
Guarantee in case of Abnormally Low Bids, the same should be

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 122 :
taken only with the approval of the next higher authority competent
to finalise the particular tender, or the Secretary of the
Ministry/Department, whichever is lower.
12. That apart, the Micro and Small Enterprises and the firms
registered with concerned Ministries/Departments were exempted
from submission of bid security, and further in lieu of bid security,
Ministries/Departments were given liberty to ask bidders to sign ‘Bid
Security Declaration’ accepting that if they withdraw or modify their
bids during period of validity etc., they will be suspended for the
time specified in the tender documents. Accordingly, it was directed
that no provisions regarding the Bid Security should be kept in the
Bid Documents of the Micro and Small Enterprises in future and only
provision for Bid Security Declaration should be kept in the bid
documents. It was also directed that wherever there are compelling
circumstances to ask for Bid Security, the same should be done
only with the approval of the next higher authority competent to
finalise the particular tender, or the Secretary of the
Ministry/Department, whichever is lower.
13. Later, as per a memorandum No. F 9/4/2020-PPD dated
30.12.2021 issued by the Government of India, the validity of the
OM regarding the “Performance Security” was extended till
31.03.2023. Therefore, on an analysis of the Office Memoranda

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 123 :
issued by the Government of India, it is clear that certain
relaxations were granted; but, the same were granted by securing
additional declarations from the contractors who are entering into
different kinds of contract. It is true that in the Government of
India Notification, Additional Performance Guarantee was
completely exempted. But, it is interesting to note that in the Office
Memorandum dated 30.12.2021 by which relaxations were
extended upto 31.03.2023, the relaxation was granted only in
respect of “Performance Security”.
14. It is equally important to note that nowhere in the Office
Memoranda issued by the Government of India, direction was
issued to the State Government to grant relaxation in Performance
Security, Additional Guarantee, Earnest Money Deposit etc. But,
apparently, the State Government, taking into consideration the
Office Memoranda issued by the Government of India, have granted
relaxations as per the State Government Orders referred to above.
15. As I have pointed out above, even though as per the
Government Order dated 07.01.2021, Additional Performance
Guarantee was insisted upon by way of 50% treasury deposit and
50% bank guarantee in cases where the quote was between 11% to
25% below the estimate rate, when the order dated 15.03.2022
was issued by the State Government extending the period upto

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 124 :
31.03.2023, the relaxation granted to the Performance Guarantee
alone was retained by the State Government.
16. In my considered opinion, the same was done by the
State Government taking into account the office Memorandum
dated 30.12.2021 issued by the Government of India, Ministry of
Finance, Department of Expenditure Procurement Policy Division
extending the validity of OM dated 12.11.2020 regarding
“Performance Security” till 31.03.2023 in the matter of direct
procurement policy.
17. The paramount contention advanced by the petitioners is
that the State Government Order dated 15.03.2022 is violative of
the directives issued by the Government of India. It is further
contended that as per the said Government Order, relaxation given
by the Government of India in the Office Memorandum referred to
above and the State Government Order dated 07.01.2021 have
been completely overlooked. It is further contended that the bidding
was done by the petitioners under a legitimate expectation that
they need not take additional burden, since they are entitled for the
benefits of the Government Order dated 07.01.2021.
18. It is also contended that as per the Government Order
dated 15.03.2022, unforeseen liability has been cast upon the
petitioners and the respondents are not entitled to impose any

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 125 :
such liability, since it is an additional burden on the petitioners,
which affects the agreed profit also. Various other contentions are
also raised.
19. The Kerala Water Authority has filed a common counter
affidavit in the writ petitions, basically contending that the Water
Authority has invited tenders from the contractors specifically
prescribing the requirements that have to be followed by the
contractors if and when they are selected to execute the
agreement. It is further contended that in response to the Notice
Inviting Tender published by the Water Authority, the petitioners
have submitted their bids along with covering letters, wherein it is
specifically stated that the petitioners have read and understood all
the conditions of the tender documents in full spirit and agrees to
accept all the conditions put forth in the tender documents without
any deviation or with exceptions.
20. The petitioners further undertook to agree to all
conditions in the Notice Inviting Tender and any other conditions
prevailing in the Kerala Water Authority. It is also contended that
once the petitioners have participated in the contract, fully knowing
well the terms and conditions to be complied with, if they are
selected, they are not at liberty to turn around and attack the
provisions of the Notice Inviting Tender. The principles of law

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 126 :
evolved by the Apex Court in various judgments are relied upon by
the Kerala Water Authority. That apart, it is contended that the
challenge made against the requirement of deposit of Additional
Performance Guarantee is liable to be rejected, since there is no
vested or contractual right to the writ petitioners, as there is no
contract entered into by and between the parties.
21. It is important to note that in the Notice Inviting Tenders,
it was specifically stated that the bidders are liable to submit
Additional Performance Guarantee for all the low quoted items and
Additional Performance Guarantee is the additional amount to be
deposited for the work quoted below the estimated rate. It is also
the contention of the Water Authority that wherever the contract in
question is an Item Rate Contract, Additional Performance
Guarantee is to be furnished for each item quoted by the bidder,
and not as contended by the petitioners that only if the total
amount quoted is below 10% of the PAC, then only additional
Performance Guarantee need be furnished.
22. That apart, it is contended that by virtue of Regulation
5(3) of the Kerala Water Authority (Authentication of Orders,
Assurance of Property and Execution of Contracts) Regulations,
1992, the PWD Code has been made applicable to the contracts
entered into by and between the contractors and the Kerala Water

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 127 :
Authority. It is further submitted that in clause 2006 of the PWD
Manual, 2021, it is stipulated that in the case of item wise
contracts, the schedule shall contain only the specification for items
and quantities. The sum and substance of the contention is that the
Kerala Water Authority is bound to adhere to the stipulations
contained in the PWD Manual. Therefore, it is submitted that two
terms are used in the Notice Inviting Tender with specific meanings
attached namely ‘Probable Amount of Contract’ and ‘equated rates’,
which means ‘the rate quoted against each item’.
23. It is also pointed out that the rate quoted by the bidder
against each item is applied against the rates used to work out the
total PAC of the amount, and the additional PAC has been worked
out. Therefore, it is submitted that whenever the petitioners quoted
very low rates for a number of items, predominantly that are to be
executed towards the end of the project and therefore, the bids are
front loaded and in order to secure assurance measure with respect
to the performance and complete the project executing all the items
mentioned in the agreement, additional Performance Guarantee was
insisted upon. It is further pointed out that an Additional
Performance Guarantee is sought in such cases in order to protect
the interest of the public and for the safe conclusion of the contract
in terms of the agreement executed by and between the parties.

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 128 :
 24. In the Statement filed for and on behalf of the Government
of India, it is unequivocally stated that the memoranda issued by it
would not bind the State Government, rather they apply only to the
Central Ministries, its Departments and Central Public Sector
Undertakings. It is also specified that the said aspect is clearly
stated in the referred memorandums.
25. The elaborate discussion of facts made above would
make it clear that the prime contention advanced by the petitioners
is that the Office memoranda issued by the Government of India in
respect of various kinds of contracts during the pandemic Covid-19
is binding on the State Government. In my considered opinion, the
same cannot be sustained under law.
26. As I have pointed out above, on an analysis of the Office
Memoranda successively issued by the Government of India with
respect to different kinds of contract, it is categoric and clear that
they are intended for the use of the Central Government
Departments and Public Sector Undertakings. It is true, the State
Government, taking into account the pandemic Covid-19 situation,
has risen to the occasion and granted relaxations in the matter of
Performance Guarantee, Additional Performance Guarantee and
other Bid Securities. But, in the subsequent extension order dated
15.03.2022, the relaxation granted was only in respect of the

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 129 :
Performance Guarantee to the extent mentioned in the State
Government Notification dated 07.01.2021 and nothing else.
27. It is relevant and important to note that the tenders were
invited by the Kerala Water Authority clearly specifying the
requirements in the Notice Inviting Tender and the Government
Orders referred to therein. Therefore, I am of the view that after
participating in the tender with open eyes, the petitioners cannot
turn around and contend that they are not liable to furnish
Additional Performance Guarantee in terms of the provisions of the
Notice Inviting Tender and the applicable State Government orders.
28. I am of the considered opinion that when tenders are
invited taking into consideration the manner in which the item wise
rates of contract are to be tackled by the Government Departments
and other Public Sector Undertakings, the Kerala Water Authority
was entitled to insist the successful bidders to execute the
agreement in terms of the Notice Inviting Tenders and other
Government orders. Even though in some writ petitions, the
petitioners have a contention that in the Notice Inviting Tender, the
item wise rate schedule was not incorporated, it is clear that the
State Government have issued notification dated 28.09.2015 in that
regard, which was later modified by the Government Orders dated
07.12.2019, by which the manner in which the Additional

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 130 :
Performance Guarantee is to be required for the work quoted below
the estimate rate, and quoted, between 11% to 25% below the
estimate rate, is prescribed.
29. As per the said Government Order dated 07.12.2019, the
Government modified G.O.(P) No. 19/2016/FIN. dated 03.02.2016
and G.O.(P) No. 124/2016/FIN. dated 29.08.2016, as per which
“(1) for the Percentage Rate Contracts—if the quoted amount of
lowest bidder (L1) is X% below the estimated PAC, the Additional
Performance Guarantee amount shall be (X-10)% of estimated PAC;
and (2) for Items rate contracts—if the rate quoted by the bidder
for an item of work is ‘X’% below the estimate cost where ‘X’ lies
above 10%, the additional Performance Guarantee for that item of
work is equal to (X-10%) of the estimate amount for that item of
work. The total Additional Performance Guarantee for the whole
work is the total individual Additional Performance Guarantee for
each item of work calculated as above.”
Therefore, it can be seen that by virtue of the said notification, the
Kerala Water Authority was entitled to insist for furnishing of the
Additional Performance Guarantee in terms of the modified
Government Order.

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 131 :
Batch II:
30. The Batch II writ petitions relate to the claims raised by
the micro, small and medium enterprises in terms of the
Orders/Notification issued by the State Government and the
Government of India, Ministry of Micro, Small and Medium
Enterprises,in the matter of awarding of contract by giving
facilities/benefits to MSMEs.
31. The basic contention advanced by the writ
petitioners/contractors is that the Government of India,Batch II as
per its Notification, F. No. 25(2)/2011-MA dated 14.10.2011,
granted the following facilities/benefits in the matter of Government
purchase to help Micro and Small Enterprises (MSEs) registered with
National Small Industries Corporation (NSIC), in marketing their
products by providing facilities/benefits:
1. Issue of tender sets free of cost;
2. Exemption from payment of Earnest Money;
3. Waiver of Security Deposit upto the Monetary Limit for which
at the Unit is registered;
4. Price Preference upto 15% over the quotation of large scale
units; and
5. In addition to the above, 358 items are reserved for exclusive
purchase from MSE Sector.

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 132 :
32. The first question to be considered is the implication of
the said Notification issued by the Ministry of Micro, Small and
Medium Enterprises, Government of India. On a perusal of the
Notification issued by the Government of India, it is clear that the
same applies to the purchase of goods from the MSEs and not in
civil contracts floated by the Government or the Public Sector
Undertakings. It is also clear that the said notification happened to
be issued on the basis of certain directions issued by the High Court
of Delhi as well as the High Court of Kerala with respect to the
purchases made from the MSEs.
33. It is true, insofar as the purchase of goods and services
are concerned, the Government of India Notification may come into
play and the State Government may be bound to grant such
benefits in the matter of procurement of goods and services by
virtue of the consequential notification issued by the State
Government. In fact, the State Government has issued a
Government Order, G.O.(P) No. 1/2021/FIN. dated 03.01.2021,
clarifying that aspect and it is directed that exemption regarding
Earnest Money Deposit/Security Deposit/ Performance Guarantee
granted to MSMEs as per Government Order, G.O.(P) No.
546/2013/Fin. dated 07.11.2013 is not applicable for civil/
Electrical/infrastructure contracts in the light that Earnest Money

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 133 :
Deposit/Security Deposit/Performance Guarantee play a vital role in
civil works in confirming satisfactory execution of contract over a
considerable time period as well as in ensuring quality of work.
34. The said question was considered by the Apex Court in
Airport Authority of India v. Centre for Aviation Policy,
Safety & Research (CAPSR) & Others [2022 SCC Online SC
1334], wherein it was held that Ground Handling Services is not
“procurement of goods” under MSME; compliance to MSME
Procurement Policy is required only after the other eligibility criteria
is fulfilled by the candidates. It was further held that insofar as the
submission with respect to MSME orders of 2012 and 2018 is
concerned, the same can always be subject to the fulfillment of
other conditions of the tender documents; that even otherwise,
selecting GHS for providing GHS cannot be equated with the
procurement of any goods and services that form the crux of the
MSME orders; and that in any case, at the instance of respondent
No. 1 therein, the High Court ought not to have entertained the writ
petition challenging the terms and conditions of the tender
documents and none of the tender conditions/eligibility criteria can
be said to be arbitrary and/or mala fide and/or actuated by bias.
35. A Division Bench of the Orissa High Court had considered

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 134 :
this question in Jalswal Plastic Tubes Ltd. and Ors. v. State of
Orissa and Ors. [MANU/OR/0368/2012) and held that the if the
tender is floated for execution of work on turnkey basis, it is not a
tender for procurement of PVC pipes from any manufacturer or
supplier and therefore, various IPRs including IPR, 2007 and the
Orissa MSME Development Policy, 2009 have no application insofar
as the work intended to be executed on turnkey basis under
Annexure-1 is concerned.
36. The petitioners have challenged the State Government
Order contending that it is in violation of the provisions of the Micro,
Small and Medium Enterprises Development Act, 2006 (‘Act, 2006’
for short) and the Notification issued by the Government of India.
However, in my considered opinion, as has been pointed out above,
the Notification issued by the Ministry of Micro, Small and Medium
Enterprises dated 14.10.2011 itself is in the context of procurement
of goods from MSEs. To put it differently, merely because
procurement of goods and services is a component factor for
execution of a civil contract, that can never be treated as a
procurement contract to secure the advantage of the orders and
notifications referred to above. Even though the learned counsel for
the petitioners submitted that the State Government Order is in
violation of the Act, 2006, I could not locate any provision in the

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 135 :
said Act to arrive at a conclusion that the State Government Order
dated 03.01.2021 would interfere with any of the provisions of the
Act, 2006.
37. Anyhow, in some of the cases filed by the MSME
contractors, a contention is advanced with respect to the benefit of
the Notifications issued by the State Government referred to in the
case of the covid related exemptions that ‘(X-10)% policy’ would
apply to the MSMEs also and therefore, the Kerala Water Authority,
in the matter of item wise rate contracts can only insist for
Additional Performance Guarantee, if the quoted amount in total is
less than 10% of the Probable Amount of Contract.
38. The said question, in fact, is answered by me in the first
batch of cases on the basis of the modification order issued by the
State Government in the year 2019 and that the ‘(X-10)% policy’
would apply to each item of component material used in the
execution of the works of the Kerala Water Authority. Even though
the petitioners have a contention that the order issued by the State
Government in the year 2019 is not applicable to the Kerala Water
Authority, I am of the view that even though the Kerala Water
Authority is a statutory body, it cannot be treated as one detached
from the State Government. At the most, it could be treated as a

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 136 :
Department of the State Government functioning under a statute.
39. Therefore, the PWD Manual and the State Government
Orders issued in the context would apply to the Kerala Water
Authority, which are also specifically mentioned in the Notice
inviting tenders. The said condition is insisted upon by the Kerala
Water Authority to ensure financial capacity of the contractors; and
to ensure that the works are not stalled during the period of
contract and any extended period granted to the contractors.
40. Moreover, the execution of civil contracts are entirely
different from procurement of goods and services; and the risk
element involved in the execution of civil works, insofar as the State
Government and its instrumentalities are concerned, is more in
nature due to various factors; specifically in the event of
abandoning the work by the contractors. Therefore, in my
considered opinion, in civil contracts, the State Government is fully
justified and legally correct asking the contractors to furnish
additional performance guarantee.
Batch III:
41. These writ petitions are filed challenging the Additional
Performance Guarantee sought for by the Kerala Water Authority in

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 137 :
respect of the Items Rate contracts. In the said writ petitions also,
other contentions with respect to the covid related benefits in the
matter of execution of contracts are raised; however, they are
dealt with in the first batch of writ petitions.
42. The basic contention advanced by the petitioners is that
in the Notice Inviting Tender, the Kerala Water Authority has not
provided the rates of each item of work and further that in the
Notification issued by the State Government with respect to the ‘(X-10)% policy’ issued in the year 2016, the State Government has not
specifically stated that it would apply to the Items Rates contracts.
As I have pointed out above, in the year 2019, the State
Government has issued the order, G.O.(P) No. 168/2019/FIN. dated
07.12.2019, as per which the Government Orders issued in the year
2016 were modified and clearly specified that for Percentage Rate
Contracts, if the quoted amount of lowest bidder(L1) is X% below
the estimated PAC, the Additional Performance Guarantee amount
shall be (X-10)% of the estimated PAC.
43. But, in the matter of Items Rates Contract, it was
clarified in the said Government Order dated 07.12.2019 that if the
rate quoted by the bidder for an item of work is ‘X%” below
estimate cost where ‘X’ lies above 10%, the Additional Performance

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 138 :
Guarantee for that item of work is equal to (X-10)% of the estimate
amount for that item of work; and the total of Additional
Performance Guarantee for the whole work is the total of individual
Additional Performance Guarantee for each item of work calculated
as above.
44. Therefore, it can be seen that the claims raised by the
petitioners absolutely on the basis of the Government Orders of the
year 2016 cannot be sustained, in view of the modified order issued
in the year 2019, which is applicable to the Kerala Water Authority,
especially when it is specified in the Notice inviting tender. That
apart, as rightly contended by the Kerala Water Authority, since
PWD Code applies to the Kerala Water Authority, it has to adhere to
the stipulations contained under the PWD Code; and the Notice
Inviting Tenders are to be issued in terms of the provisions of the
PWD Code. The manner in which the contracts are to be invited and
the conditions are to be stipulated in the Notice Inviting Tender are
clearly specified in the PWD Code, by which the Kerala Water
Authority needs to issue the Notice Inviting Tender in accordance
with the parameters contained under the PWD Code.
45. Therefore, merely because the items are shown in the
schedule and the rates are not prescribed, that would not ensure to

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 139 :
the benefit of the contractors from quoting the rates for individual
items, taking into account the estimated Probable Amount of
Contract. Considering the facts and figures, I am of the view that if
the contracts are invited by the Kerala Water Authority on the basis
of the Percentage Rate Contracts, then the contractors are entitled
to provide Additional Performance Guarantee only on the basis of
Probable Amount of Contract. But, if the tenders are invited on the
basis of the ‘items rate wise’, then the contractors are duty bound
to provide Additional Performance Guarantee for each item of work
calculated on the basis of (X-10)% for the estimated amount for
that item of work.
46. In one of the writ petitions, a latest tender issued by the
Kerala Water Authority is produced, wherein, in the Items Rates
contract, the Schedule of rates are shown and therefore, according
to the learned counsel for the petitioner therein, the Water
Authority has realised the situation now and it was accordingly that
schedule of rates are included. But, in my considered opinion,
pursuant to the order issued by the State Government in the year
2019, even if the Water Authority has not shown the rates for each
item, it was for the contractors to offer the same showing the items
wise rates. Therefore, merely because the Water Authority has
issued a schedule of items wise rate contract in a tender invited in

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 140 :
the year 2023, that will not enure to the benefit of the contractors
to contend that the contractors were not liable to provide items
wise rates, even due to the modified order issued by the State
Government in the year 2019.
47. In W.P.(C). No. 6964 of 2023 included in ‘Batch III’, the
petitioner seeks a direction to the respondents to release the Bank
Guarantee furnished by the petitioner towards Additional
Performance Guarantee. The said relief is sought for by the
petitioner on the basis of the Government Order dated 07.12.2019
in the matter of tenders furnished below the 10% of the estimated
PAC. In view of the findings rendered above, the petitioner herein is
also liable to furnish Additional Performance Guarantee, if the
tenders are invited on the basis of the Items Rates Contract.
Therefore, the petitioner is not entitled to get any reliefs in respect
of the Additional Performance Guarantee insisted upon. The findings
rendered above would apply to the petitioner in this writ petition as
well.
48. It is a well settled law in the realm of contract that the
bidder, having participated in the tender accepting the conditions
and requirements contained in the Notice Inviting Tender, cannot
seek equity and protection of the fundamental rights guaranteed
under Articles 14 and 19(1)(g) of the Constitution of India, unless

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 141 :
there is patent arbitrariness, irrationality, mala fides, nepotism and
other similar situations. It is also well settled in law that unless and
until gross illegality, mala fides, arbitrariness or irrationality are
established, the writ court would be slow to interfere in the terms
and conditions of a contract or a Notice Inviting Tender. After all,
the contracts in question are all commercial contracts, the terms of
which were recited in advance by the Kerala Water Authority
through its Notice Inviting Tenders and the references made
thereto. A reference to a few decisions of the Apex Court would
make the situation more clear.
49. In Global Energy Ltd. v. Adani Exports Ltd., (2005) 4
SCC 435], it was held that the terms of the invitation to tender are
not open to judicial scrutiny and the courts cannot whittle down the
terms of the tender as they are in the realm of contract unless they
are wholly arbitrary, discriminatory or actuated by malice.
50. In Master Marine Services (P) Ltd. v. Metcalfe &
Hodgkinson (P) Ltd., (2005) 6 SCC 138], it was held that the
court does not have the expertise to correct the administrative
decision and if a review of the administrative decision is permitted,
it will be substituting its own decision, without the necessary
expertise, which itself may be fallible. It was further held that the
Government must have freedom of contract and that fair play in the

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 142 :
joints is a necessary concomitant for an administrative body
functioning in an administrative sphere or quasi-administrative
sphere.
51. In Airport Authority of India v. Centre for Aviation
Policy, Safety and Research (CAPSR) and Ors. [AIR 2022 SC
4749], it was held that while exercising the powers under Article
226 of the Constitution of India, the terms and conditions of the
Invitation to Tender cannot be set aside. It was further held that as
per the settled position of law, the terms and conditions of the
Invitation to Tender are within the domain of the tenderer/tender
making authority and are not open to judicial scrutiny, unless they
are arbitrary, discriminatory or mala fide. It is further held that the
bidders participating in the tender process cannot, therefore, insist
that their tenders should be accepted simply because a given tender
is the highest or lowest depending upon whether the contract is for
sale of public property or for execution of works on behalf of the
Government; and all that participating bidders are entitled to is a
fair, equal and nondiscriminatory treatment in the matter of
evaluation of their tenders; that it is also fairly well settled that
award of a contract is essentially a commercial transaction which
must be determined on the basis of consideration that are relevant
to such commercial decision and which implies that terms subject to

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 143 :
which tenders are invited are not open to the judicial scrutiny,
unless it is found that the same have been tailor-made to benefit
any particular tenderer or class of tenderers.
52. In Michigan Rubber (India) Limited v. State of
Karnataka [(2012) 8 SCC 216] it was held that; in the matter of
formulating conditions of a tender document and awarding a
contract, greater latitude is required to be conceded to the State
authorities, unless the action of the tendering authority is found to
be malicious and a misuse of its statutory powers; interference by
courts is not warranted; and that certain preconditions or
qualifications for tenders have to be laid down to ensure that the
contractor has the capacity and the resources to successfully
execute the work; and if the State or its instrumentalities act
reasonably, fairly and in public interest in awarding contract, here
again, interference by court is very restrictive, since no person can
claim a fundamental right to carry on business with the
Government.
53. In that view of the matter, I do not think, the petitioners
are entitled to get any relief of excluding the Additional Performance
Guarantee insisted upon by the Kerala Water Authority and
therefore, the reliefs sought for by the petitioners in that regard are
declined.

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 144 :
54. But, fact remains, when the writ petitions were admitted
to the files of this Court, interim orders were granted by this Court
directing the Kerala Water Authority to execute agreements with
the petitioners on condition that the petitioners execute a simple
bond undertaking to furnish additional Performance Guarantee if
and when so directed by this Court at a later point of time. Being
so, it is important to note that, consequent to the interim orders
passed, agreements were executed and work has progressed
considerably. In some cases, I am informed that the works are
complete; in certain other cases, works have progressed above
30%, and some even attained upto 75% and above.
55. In some of the writ petitions, insistence of 50% of the
Additional Performance Guarantee by way of treasury fixed deposit
was challenged and interim orders were passed to provide 100%
bank guarantee. In fact, the said question with respect to the
validity of the condition for providing 50% of the Additional
Performance Guarantee by way of treasury Fixed deposit was
considered by a Division Bench of this Court in State of Kerala v.
Joseph Velupuzhakkal [2018(3) KLT 1076] and upheld the said
condition for furnishing 50% of the Performance Guarantee by
treasury fixed deposit.
56. In that view of the matter, I am of the opinion that, in

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 145 :
the peculiar facts and circumstances of these cases, certain general
directions can be issued. Accordingly, these writ petitions are
disposed of with the following directions:
1. If the work in accordance with the agreement is completed by
the contractors, no Additional Performance Guarantee shall be
insisted upon.
2. If the work has progressed to a level exceeding 80%, the
tendering authority is left with the liberty to take a decision as
to whether any Additional Performance Guarantee is to be
insisted upon, taking into account the efficiency, reputation,
men and machinery engaged, and other positive factors of the
respective contractors.
3. If the work has exceeded 30% of the total work, then the
tendering authority is directed to secure Additional
Performance Guarantee proportionate to the balance work.
4. In all other cases, the contractors/petitioners are directed to
furnish an Additional Performance Guarantee to the tendering
authority as specified in the tender documents within three
weeks from the date of receipt of a copy of this judgment.
5. In cases where a Performance guarantee is furnished by the
contractors in spite of its relaxation up to 31.03.2023, the
said amounts can be adjusted against the Additional
Performance guarantee required in the circumstances
specified above.

W.P.(C) No. 14051/2022 & batch
: 146 :
6. In cases where 100% bank guarantee is furnished, instead of
50% by way of treasury fixed deposit, the same is permitted
to be continued in the facts and circumstances, if the work
has exceeded 30% of the total work.
7. If the tenders are invited on the basis of the Percentage Rate
Contracts, then the contractors are liable to furnish the
Additional Performance Guarantee on the basis of (X-10)% of
the estimated PAC.
8. If the tenders are invited on the basis of Items Rates
Contract, then the contractors are liable to furnish Additional
Performance Guarantee for each item of work on the basis of
(X-10)% of the estimated amount for each item of work.
9. I make it clear that the directions are issued as above taking
into account the peculiar facts, features and circumstances
involved in these cases consequent to the interim orders
granted; and it shall not be treated as a precedent, except in
regard to the writ petitions pending in the above context.
 sd/- SHAJI P. CHALY, JUDGE.

Rv
